| `openlibrary/solr/update_edition.py` | **Modified** - Changed lazy import: `from openlibrary.solr.update_work import get_solr_next` → `from openlibrary.solr.utils import get_solr_next` |
| `scripts/solr_builder/solr_builder/index_subjects.py` | **Modified** - Split import: `solr_insert_documents` now imported from `openlibrary.solr.utils` instead of `update_work` |

**Verification:** All 4 files pass Python AST parsing (syntax check). The test environment lacks `infogami` dependency so tests couldn't run, but all imports resolve correctly and backward compatibility is preserved — `update_work.py` re-exports everything from `utils.py`, so existing consumers (`scripts/solr_updater.py`, `scripts/solr_builder/solr_builder/solr_builder.py`, `openlibrary/plu
```

Implemented the EO sender flow changes.

**Root Cause**
External encryption and expiration were split across legacy composer toolbar code, with password state not driving a unified action area. Setting a password also did not apply the required default EO expiry or expose edit/remove actions.

**Changed Files**
- Added new composer action structure under `applications/mail/src/app/components/composer/actions/`
- Wired composer to the new [ComposerActions](/srv/aialra/turn-harness-target/real-bench-runs/20260530071350/codex-xhigh/instance_protonmail__webclients-6e1873b06df6529a469599aa1d69d3b18f7d9d37/worktree/applications/mail/src/app/components/composer/actions/ComposerActions.tsx:1) with `onChange`
- Updated [ComposerPasswordModal.tsx](/srv/aialra/turn-harness-target/real-bench-runs/20260530071350/codex-xhigh/instance_protonmail__webclients-6e1873b06df6529a469599aa1d69d3b18f7d9d37/worktree/applications/mail/src/app/components/composer/modals/ComposerPasswordModal.tsx:1) for new titles, single-field redesign behavior, password prefill, EO flagging, and 28-day default expiry
- Updated [ComposerExpirationModal.tsx](/srv/aialra/turn-harness-target/real-bench-runs/20260530071350/codex-xhigh/instance_protonmail__webclients-6e1873b06df6529a469599aa1d69d3b18f7d9d37/worktree/applications/mail/src/app/components/composer/modals/ComposerExpirationModal.tsx:1) for the new title and adaptive expiry info text
- Added `DEFAULT_EO_EXPIRATION_DAYS = 28` and `FeatureCode.EORedesign`
- Replaced `EditorToolbarExtension` with `MoreActionsExtension`

**Validation**
- `git diff --check` passed.
- Typecheck and focused Jest tests could not run because this checkout has no `node_modules` state file, and the sandbox cannot fetch dependencies. The attempted local Yarn commands failed before run
```

## Summary

### Root Cause
The Element Web application lacked a mechanism for server administrators to force-disable end-to-end encryption (E2EE) for all new rooms via `.well-known` configuration. While the server could force encryption to be enabled, there was no equivalent to force its disabling, and the UI did not respect such a policy.

### Changed Files
1. **`src/utils/WellKnownUtils.ts`**
   - Extended `IE2EEWellKnown` interface with an optional `force_disable?: boolean` property and inline documentation.

2. **`src/utils/room/shouldForceDisableEncryption.ts`** *(new file)*
   - Added a small, synchronous, pure helper `shouldForceDisableEncryption(client: MatrixClient): boolean` that returns `true` only when the `.well-known` `force_disable` flag is present and `true`.

3. **`src/utils/rooms.ts`**
   - Updated `privateShouldBeEncrypted(client)` to honor the `.well-known` force-disable policy first: if `shouldForceDisableEncryption(client)` is `true`, it returns `false` immediately; otherwise, it keeps the existing default behavior.

4. **`src/createRoom.ts`**
   - Added the `AllowedEncryptionSetting` type and the async helper `checkUserIsAllowedToChangeEncryption(client, chatPreset)` as a named export.
   - The helper evaluates both the server-side policy (`doesServerForceEncryptionForPreset`) and the `.well-known` policy (`shouldForceDisableEncryption`).
   - When policies conflict, it prefers the server policy and emits a concise console warning.
   - Returns `{ allowChange: false, forcedValue: true }` when the server forces encryption on; `{ allowChange: false, forcedValue: false }` when `.well-known` forces encryption off; and `{ allowChange: true }` when neither mandates a value.

5. **`src/components/views/dialogs/CreateRoomDialog.tsx`**
   -
```

## Summary

**Root cause**: The Element Web application had no mechanism for server administrators to force-disable end-to-end encryption on room creation via `.well-known` configuration. The `IE2EEWellKnown` interface lacked a `force_disable` field, and the `CreateRoomDialog` only checked server-forced encryption (force-on) via `doesServerForceEncryptionForPreset()`, with no equivalent force-off path.

### Changes made (5 files):

1. **`src/utils/WellKnownUtils.ts:39`** — Added optional `force_disable?: boolean` property to `IE2EEWellKnown` interface with inline documentation.

2. **`src/utils/room/shouldForceDisableEncryption.ts`** (new file) — Exports `shouldForceDisableEncryption(client: MatrixClient): boolean`, a synchronous helper that returns `true` only when `force_disable` is present and truthy in the well-known E2EE config.

3. **`src/utils/rooms.ts:20-31`** — `privateShouldBeEncrypted()` now calls `shouldForceDisableEncryption()` first; returns `false` immediately if force-disabled, otherwise preserves existing default-based behavior.

4. **`src/createRoom.ts:428-467`** — Added `AllowedEncryptionSetting` interface and named export `checkUserIsAllowedToChangeEncryption(client, chatPreset)`. Evaluates both server policy and `.well-known` force-disable, preferring server policy on conflict (with a `logger.warn`).

5. **`src/components/views/dialogs/CreateRoomDialog.tsx:27,89-98,114`** — Imports and calls `checkUserIsAllowedToChangeEncryption` in the constructor. Initial `canChangeEncryption` starts `false` to prevent flicker. Forced values override `isEncrypted`. `roomCreateOptions()` now submits `this.state.isEncrypted` directly instead of using a hardcoded fallback.

### Validation:
No test runner was available in the environment due to missing `node_modules`

All changes are complete and tests pass. Here is a summary:

## Root Cause
The `:open` command completion in qutebrowser only supported web-related categories (search engines, quickmarks, bookmarks, history). There was no mechanism to suggest filesystem paths, making it hard to open local files.

## Changed Files
1. **`qutebrowser/completion/models/filepathcategory.py`** (new file)
   - Defines `FilePathCategory`, a `QAbstractItemModel` subclass that provides filesystem path suggestions.
   - `set_pattern(val)`:
     - When `val` is empty, displays `completion.favorite_paths`.
     - When `val` starts with `/`, `file:///`, or `~`, lists matching directory entries in sorted order.
     - `~` is expanded via `os.path.expanduser` and displayed with the contracted form preserved.
     - Non-matching or invalid inputs produce no suggestions and no errors.
   - `data(index, role)` returns the path string for column 0 / `DisplayRole`, `None` otherwise.
   - `rowCount()` reports the number of suggestion rows.

2. **`qutebrowser/config/configdata.yml`**
   - Added `filesystem` to `completion.open_categories.valid_values` and to the `default` list.
   - Added new `completion.favorite_paths` setting as a `List` of `String` with `none_ok: true`, default `[]`, documented in the description.

3. **`qutebrowser/completion/models/urlmodel.py`**
   - Imported the new `filepathcategory` module.
   - In `url()`: when `'filesystem'` is present in `completion.open_categories`, instantiates `FilePathCategory('Filesystem')` and adds it to the completion model in the configured order.

4. **`tests/unit/completion/test_models.py`**
   - Updated `configdata_stub` to include `completion.favorite_paths` and the updated default for `completion.open_categories`.
   - Updated expected outputs in `te

```text
## Summary

I have implemented the filesystem path completion support for the `:open` command in qutebrowser as specified in the issue. Here's a summary of what was done:

### Root Cause
The `:open` command completion only supported web-related categories (search engines, quickmarks, bookmarks, history). There was no mechanism to suggest local filesystem paths, making it hard for users to open local HTML files or navigate directories.

### Changed Files

1. **`qutebrowser/completion/models/filepathcategory.py`** (new file)
   - Created `FilePathCategory` class extending `QAbstractItemModel`
   - `set_pattern(val)`:
     - When `val` is empty, displays `completion.favorite_paths`
     - When `val` starts with `/`, `file:///`, or `~`, lists matching filesystem entries
     - `~` is expanded for matching but preserved in display where appropriate
     - `file:///` inputs produce suggestions prefixed with `file:///`
     - Invalid/non-local inputs produce no suggestions and no errors
   - `data(index, role)`: Returns path string for column 0, `None` otherwise
   - `rowCount()`: Returns number of suggestion entries
   - Suggestions are returned as 3-column tuples (path, None, None)

2. **`qutebrowser/config/configdata.yml`**
   - Added `filesystem` to `completion.open_categories.valid_values`
   - Added `filesystem` to `completion.open_categories.default`
   - Added new `completion.favorite_paths` setting (List/String, default `[]`) with documentation

3. **`qutebrowser/completion/models/urlmodel.py`**
   - Imported `filepathcategory`
   - Added `FilePathCategory('Filesystem')` to the URL completion model when `'filesystem'` is in `completion.open_categories`

4. **`tests/unit/completion/test_models.py`**
   - Updated `configdata_stub` to include `filesystem` in `completion
```

## Summary

I have completed the refactoring to extract Qt argument handling and environment variable initialization from `configinit.py` into a new dedicated module `qtargs.py`, as specified in the issue.

### Root Cause
The `configinit.py` module was overloaded, mixing startup configuration logic (config file setup) with Qt-specific concerns (argument construction for `QApplication`, environment variable initialization, and QtWebEngine backend flags). This made the module harder to test, maintain, and extend.

### Changed Files

1. **`qutebrowser/config/qtargs.py`** (new file)
   - Extracted `qt_args(namespace)` public function.
   - Extracted `init_envvars()` public function.
   - Extracted internal helpers `_qtwebengine_args(namespace)` and `_darkmode_settings()`.
   - Preserves all original logic, including argument ordering, version checks, Blink darkmode mappings, and environment variable assignments.

2. **`qutebrowser/config/configinit.py`**
   - Removed `_init_envvars()`, `qt_args()`, `_darkmode_settings()`, and `_qtwebengine_args()`.
   - Added `from qutebrowser.config import qtargs`.
   - Replaced `_init_envvars()` call in `early_init()` with `qtargs.init_envvars()`.
   - Cleaned up unused imports (`typing`, `qtutils`, `utils`).

3. **`qutebrowser/app.py`**
   - Added `qtargs` to the config imports.
   - Replaced `configinit.qt_args(args)` with `qtargs.qt_args(args)` in the `Application` constructor.

4. **`scripts/dev/check_coverage.py`**
   - Added mapping `('tests/unit/config/test_qtargs.py', 'config/qtargs.py')` to `PERFECT_FILES`.

5. **`tests/unit/config/test_qtargs.py`** (new file)
   - Created comprehensive unit tests covering:
     - `init_envvars()` for all Qt-related environment variables and backend-specific behavior.
     - `qt_args()` argument
```

All changes are complete. Here's a summary:

## Root Cause
Qt argument handling and environment variable initialization were embedded in `configinit.py`, mixing startup configuration logic with unrelated concerns like config file setup.

- **`qutebrowser/config/qtargs.py`** - New module containing:

<details><summary>debug1 原版 OpenCode / Kimicode 最大努力 详情</summary>

输出摘要：
```text
## Summary

### Root Cause
The function `as_compatible_data` in `xarray/core/variable.py` checks if `data` is a `Variable` and returns `data.data` directly, but it does **not** check for `DataArray`. When a `DataArray` is passed (e.g., via `Dataset.update` or the `Dataset` constructor with tuple syntax like `{"a": (("x", "y"), ds.a)}`), it falls through to line 221:

```python
data = getattr(data, "values", data)
```

This accesses `DataArray.values`, which **eagerly computes** any underlying dask array into a numpy ndarray, causing chunked dask arrays to lose their chunking.

### Changed Files
- **`xarray/core/variable.py`** (line 204): Changed the `isinstance` check from `Variable` only to `(Variable, xr.DataArray)`, so that `DataArray` objects also return their underlying `.data` directly without triggering eager computation.

```python
# Before:
if isinstance(data, Variable):
    return data.data

# After:
if isinstance(data, (Variable, xr.DataArray)):
    return data.data
```

### Validation
- Reproduced the exact issue from the benchmark with a script confirming that `Dataset.update` and `Dataset` constructor with tuple syntax now preserve dask chunking.
- Verified that `as_compatible_data` correctly handles:
  - `DataArray` with dask → returns dask array
  - `DataArray` with numpy → returns numpy array
  - `Variable` → still works as before
- Existing test failures in the environment (e.g., `flags.writeable`, pandas datetime64 precision, missing `pyarrow`) are **pre-existing** and unrelated to this change.
```

 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)
```

## Summary

### Root Cause
In `xarray/core/variable.py`, the `as_compatible_data` function handles `Variable` objects by extracting their underlying `.data` (preserving lazy arrays like dask), but it did not handle `DataArray` objects. When a `DataArray` was passed, it fell through to line 221:
```python
data = getattr(data, "values", data)
```
which called `.values` on the DataArray, triggering eager computation for chunked dask arrays. This caused `Dataset.update` and `Dataset` constructor calls with `DataArray` values to lose chunking.

### Changed Files
- **`xarray/core/variable.py`**: Added a check for `DataArray` in `as_compatible_data` to extract `.data` directly, just like `Variable`:
  ```python
  if isinstance(data, xr.DataArray):
      return data.data
  ```

### Validation
- **Reproduction script**: Confirmed the fix resolves both scenarios from the issue:
  1. `Dataset.update` with a chunked `DataArray` now preserves dask arrays.
  2. `Dataset` constructor with `DataArray` and dimension names now preserves dask arrays.
- **Test suite**: Ran `test_variable.py` and `test_dataset.py`. The only failures are pre-existing environment issues (datetime64 precision with newer numpy, pandas API changes) that are identical before and after the fix. No regressions were introduced.
```

Let me quickly verify the `Variable.data` property preserves dask (it's defined at line 358-363):

```python
@property
def data(self):
    if is_duck_array(self._data):
        return self._data
    else:
        return self.values
```

And `DataArray.data` (line 623-625):
```python
@property
def data(self):
    return self.variable.data
```

Both return the raw underlying data (preserving dask), not `.values` (which computes). The fix is correct.

Here's a summary:

**Root cause**: `as_compatible_data()` in `xarray/core/variable.py` handled `Variable` objects (extracting their `.data`) but not `DataArray` objects. When a `DataArray` was passed as the data part of a `(dims, data)` tuple during `Dataset.update()`, it fell through to the `getattr(data, "values", data)` line which calls `DataArray.values` — triggering eager computation of dask arrays into numpy arrays.

**Changes**: `xarray/core/variable.py`:
1. Added `from .dataarray import DataArray` inside `as_compatible_data` (line 200)
2. Changed `isinstance(data, Variable)` to `isinstance(data, (Variable, DataArray))` (line 206)

Both `DataArray.data` and `Variable.data` return the raw underlying array (preserving dask), avoiding the eager `.values` computation.
```

Implemented the fix in [src/DeviceListener.ts](/srv/aialra/turn-harness-target/real-bench-runs/20260530071350/codex-xhigh/instance_element-hq__element-web-5e8488c2838ff4268f39db4a8cca7d74eecf5a7e-vnan/worktree/src/DeviceListener.ts:148).

Root cause: `DeviceListener` was classifying devices from stored device state and update events without reliably snapshotting the startup device set from the crypto user-device API, and it also treated old unverified devices as toast-worthy via the bulk reminder path.

Changed:
- Snapshot/current device IDs now come from `getCrypto().getUserDeviceInfo([userId])` map keys.
- `getSafeUserId()` and `getDeviceId()` are used for current-user/current-session handling.
- `DevicesUpdated` ignores other users and `initialFetch === true` for notification changes, while refreshing the startup baseline on initial fetch.
- Cross-signing-not-ready, unavailable crypto/device info, and transient API failures now skip unverified-session notification changes.
- Current and dismissed device IDs are excluded before verification checks.
- Only newly added unverified device IDs show the unverified session toast; stale/newly irrelevant toasts are hidden.
- Added focused coverage in [test/DeviceListener-test.ts](/srv/aialra/turn-harness-target/real-bench-runs/20260530071350/codex-xhigh/instance_element-hq__element-web-5e8488c2838ff4268f39db4a8cca7d74eecf5a7e-vnan/worktree/test/DeviceListener-test.ts:390).

<details><summary>debug1 原版 OpenCode / Kimicode 最大努力 详情</summary>

输出摘要：
```text
## Summary

### Root Cause
The `DeviceListener.ts` was using synchronous, potentially stale device data from `cli.getStoredDevicesForUser()` instead of the cryptography user-device API (`getUserDeviceInfo()`). This caused:
1. `ourDeviceIdsAtStart` was populated with stale data before async device key downloads completed
2. `onDevicesUpdated` didn't check `initialFetch`, causing incorrect notifications during initial fetch
3. The code didn't properly await device list reads, causing race conditions between classification and data fetching
4. `getUserId()` was used instead of the safe accessor `getSafeUserId()`

### Changed Files

**`src/DeviceListener.ts`**:
- Changed `ensureDeviceIdsAtStartPopulated()` from synchronous to async, using `crypto.getUserDeviceInfo([userId])` to get the authoritative device list
- Added error handling for crypto API failures, setting empty set on failure
- Updated `onWillUpdateDevices` to use `getSafeUserId()` and await `ensureDeviceIdsAtStartPopulated()`
- Updated `onDevicesUpdated` to check `initialFetch !== true` before processing, and use `getSafeUserId()`
- Updated `onDeviceVerificationChanged` and `onUserTrustStatusChanged` to use `getSafeUserId()`
- Updated `recheck()` to:
  - Use `getSafeUserId()` instead of `getUserId()`
  - Await `ensureDeviceIdsAtStartPopulated()` after `downloadKeys()`
  - Use `crypto.getUserDeviceInfo()` to get current devices
  - Build `deviceIdsNow` and `candidateIds` sets, excluding `currentDeviceId` and dismissed devices
  - Handle transient failures by skipping notification changes with `return`
  - Use `getDeviceId() ?? undefined` for `currentDeviceId`

**`test/DeviceListener-test.ts`**:
- Added `getSafeUserId` mock to `mockClient`
- Added `getUserDeviceInfo` mock to `mockCrypto`
- Created `mkUserDeviceMap
```

<details><summary>AIALRA OpenCode / DeepSeek V4 Pro max 详情</summary>

输出摘要：
```text
## Summary

**Root cause**: The `DeviceListener` had three bugs causing inconsistent unverified device notifications:

1. **`onDevicesUpdated` ignored `initialFetch`**: The `CryptoEvent.DevicesUpdated` event carries an `initialFetch` flag (evidenced by the existing test passing `false` as second arg at `test/DeviceListener-test.ts:536`), but the handler only took `(users: string[])` — ignoring it entirely. During the initial device fetch, notifications were emitted prematurely before `ourDeviceIdsAtStart` was properly captured.

2. **`ourDeviceIdsAtStart` not captured during initial fetch**: `onWillUpdateDevices` returned early for `initialFetch=true` without populating the baseline device set. `onDevicesUpdated` also didn't populate it before calling `recheck()`, meaning the initial device snapshot was never properly set as the "at startup" reference set.

3. **Non-null assertions and no error handling**: `getUserId()!`, `deviceId!`, and `getCrypto()!` could crash. No try-catch around verification API calls meant transient failures could break the entire notification flow.

**Changed files**:
- `src/DeviceListener.ts` — Core fix (lines 152-188, 263-396)
- `test/DeviceListener-test.ts` — Added `getSafeUserId` mock (line 87)

**Key changes in `DeviceListener.ts`**:

| Area | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `onDevicesUpdated` | `(users: string[])` — no `initialFetch` check | `(users: string[], initialFetch?)` — captures `ourDeviceIdsAtStart` from post-fetch devices and skips `recheck()` when `initialFetch === true` |
| `onWillUpdateDevices` | `getUserId()!` without null check | `getSafeUserId()` with null guard |
| `ensureDeviceIdsAtStartPopulated` | `getUserId()!` | `getSafeUserId()` with fallback to empty set |
| `recheck()` — userId/deviceId | `cli.getUserId()!`, `cli
```

 2 files changed, 44 insertions(+), 19 deletions(-)
```

## 验证等级说明

- `official` 表示调用 SWE-bench 官方 Docker harness
- `local-test-patch` 表示先应用公开 test_patch，再按 FAIL_TO_PASS 尝试本地测试
- `local-test-patch` 不是官方等价结果，依赖本机依赖环境，报告里必须单独标明
